But the weird thing was, I didn't feel tired. I'd wake up feeling totally fine, actually. I just figured, well, I'm awake, I might as well use the extra time to get ahead on work and everything else. It just felt normal at the time, but looking back, it's probably not.\n\nDoctor: Between these periods of high energy and the current month, have you had times where your mood was unusually low or depressed, or where you lost interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely have those times. Um, lately, actually, I'll just wake up with this... this heavy, gray feeling right in my chest. \n\nIt's hard to describe, but even when the apartment is completely quiet and nothing bad is happening at all, I just feel incredibly down. Like, I feel like I could just break down and cry over seeing a coffee mug left in the sink. It's just a really, really low feeling.\n\nDoctor: When you have these periods where you wake up with that heavy, gray feeling in your chest and feel very down, how long do those episodes typically last? Are we talking about a few days, a week or more, or longer than that?\n\nPatient: Well, the heavier stretches... the really low periods... they usually last for a while. I'd say they've been showing up pretty consistently since my separation, which was about 11 months ago. \n\nIt's been going on since then, honestly. Even before this recent move I did, which just seemed to make everything feel sharper and more intense, those low periods were already there, lasting for weeks at a time.\n\nDoctor: During these low periods that last for weeks at a time, have they typically lasted at least 2 weeks each, or have they been even longer than that?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely at least two weeks. Usually, once that heavy feeling settles in, it just stays. It's not something that clears up after a few days. It's more like... I'll be stuck in that mode for a couple of weeks, sometimes longer, before I even start to feel like myself again. So, yeah, it's pretty consistently two weeks or more.\n\nDoctor: Since your separation 11 months ago, have these low periods with weeks of that heavy feeling been happening on and off with breaks in between where your mood felt more normal, or has that low mood been pretty much there the whole time with only brief breaks?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. Um... it's a little hard to look back over the last 11 months and map it all out. I know those heavy stretches last for weeks, but I haven't really kept track of whether my mood actually goes completely back to normal in between, or if that low feeling is just kind of... always there in the background. It all just sort of blurs together, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: When you had that period a few months ago where you only needed 3 or 4 hours of sleep and felt electric—was that a distinct episode that came and went, or was it more of a gradual shift out of one of those heavy, low periods you've been describing?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm really not sure how it started, to be honest. I don't know if I slowly came out of a low period or if it just... happened all at once. I just remember being in it. Like, I just realized at some point that I was running on hardly any sleep and felt really wired, but the actual transition into it? I didn't really notice how that worked.\n\nDoctor: When you think back over the last 11 months, in that period when you felt electric and needed very little sleep—did that feel like a completely different state that stood out from your usual baseline, or did it feel more like a brief uptick while you were still basically in your normal mood range?\n\nPatient: It definitely stood out. It didn't feel like my normal baseline at all. Like, earlier this month when that was going on, I felt almost electric and really upbeat one minute... but then the next minute, I'd just snap at a contractor I work with, or my sister, over these really tiny delays. It was like I felt they were personally trying to block me or slow me down. So, yeah, it was a completely different state, not just a normal good mood.\n\nDoctor: When you had that period a few months ago where you felt electric, needed very little sleep, and were getting things done—how many days did that state last before it started to shift or change?\n\nPatient: I'm... really not sure exactly how many days it lasted. It's hard to put a number on it. Like I said, it all kind of blurs together for me. I just remember being in that state and then eventually not being in it anymore, but I didn't count the days.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to that time a few months ago when you felt electric and were running on very little sleep—do you think that state lasted for at least 4 or 5 days in a row, or was it shorter than that?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't say for sure. Like I mentioned, the days kind of just blurred into each other. I know it lasted for a bit, but whether it was exactly four or five days, or maybe a little less... I just didn't track it closely enough to give you a solid answer on that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at that time a few months ago when you felt electric and needed very little sleep—if you had to estimate, would you say that state lasted somewhere around a week or so, or do you think it was more like just a couple of days?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to pin down a timeline, but... I honestly still don't know. It might have been around a week, I guess? Or maybe it was just a few days. \n\nWhen I get in that zone, especially if I'm deep into a design project, time just sort of stops meaning anything. I'm sorry, I just really can't say one way or the other.\n\nDoctor: I know it's hard to remember exactly, but thinking back to that time when you felt electric and barely needed any sleep—do you remember whether you felt that way for several days in a row, or was it really just one or two days before things shifted?\n\nPatient: I... I really just don't know. I mean, it was definitely more than a day, but beyond that, I just don't have a clear memory of how long it went on. I'm sorry, I'm just guessing at this point. I really can't give you a clear answer on the exact number of days.\n\nDoctor: During that time a few months ago when you felt electric and barely slept—do you remember whether you were able to go to work or handle your normal daily responsibilities the whole time, or did things get so intense that you had to step back or take time off?\n\nPatient: Well, I didn't take time off exactly, but... things definitely slipped. I actually missed a pretty important design review during that time. I just completely spaced on it. And honestly, since then, I've kind of been avoiding my friends and family. I just... I know it wasn't just one day, but whether it was Monday to Wednesday, or Thursday... I honestly don't know. I'm sorry, I just can't remember it clearly enough to say how many calendar days it crossed.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to that electric period a few months ago, even though the days blurred together—do you have any sense of whether you were in that state for multiple days in a row, like you could point to at least three or four different days where you felt that way, or does it feel more like it might have just been one or two days total?\n\nPatient: I know it was more than just one or two days. I just... I can't tell you if it was exactly three or four days, or maybe five. It definitely stretched on for a while, but the exact number of days in a row is just lost to me. I know it was a block of time, I just can't put a number on it.\n\nDoctor: I understand the timeline is fuzzy, but this is important: looking back at that electric period a few months ago when you barely slept—do you think it's more likely that you felt that way for at least 4 days in a row, or do you think it was probably less than 4 days total?\n\nPatient: I... I really wish I could give you a firm answer on that. If I had to guess whether it was at least four days or slightly less... I honestly just don't know. \n\nIt could have been three days, it could have been four or five. I just don't have that specific timeline in my head anymore. I can't honestly say if it was four days or if it was three. It's like trying to remember a dream—I know it happened, I know it lasted a while, but I can't count the days. I get what you're trying to do by framing it with the days of the week, but... it doesn't really change what I can remember.
